Yeah… I mean, I think so. It wasn’t like one single terrible day where everything changed overnight, but if I’m honest, it really started around eight months ago.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I wish I could say yes. Part of me wants to believe that if the work situation got fixed, if people stopped questioning me and I felt like I had my footing again, then maybe I’d snap back into myself.

But honestly… I don’t know that it would just go away. Because this feeling is there before I even get to work, before I check my phone, before anything happens. I wake up and it’s already sitting in my chest — this heavy, hollow thing. And it’s not just “I’m upset about my job,” exactly. It feels more like the old me, the Ruben who could talk to anybody, who could enjoy the day and feel comfortable in his own skin… like that guy has disappeared somehow.

So, yeah, fixing work would probably help some. I’m sure it would. But I’m scared it wouldn’t be enough, because this feels like it’s gotten inside me.

No… no, not like this. I mean, I’ve had rough stretches in life like anybody, especially with work stress here and there, but this particular feeling — this heavy, hollow thing — it’s been about eight months.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No… not really. That’s actually one of the things that scares me, because I used to be pretty easy to please, you know? I had things I looked forward to.

I used to love bowling — not even just the game, but the whole routine of it, seeing people, joking around, keeping score, talking trash a little. And the garden… I could spend half a Saturday out there messing with plants, pulling weeds, making little plans in my head about what I wanted to fix next. My wife and I would go to gallery shows sometimes, and I liked taking her, seeing her get excited about the paintings or whatever was there. We used to go dancing too, and even if I wasn’t the best dancer, I enjoyed being out with her.

Now it’s like… I just can’t get myself there. Or if I do, I’m not really there. I sit there feeling like I’m waiting for it to be over. A lot of the time I make excuses — I’m tired, I’ve got something to do, maybe next time — but the truth is, none of it feels worth the effort. And then I feel bad, because these are things that used to feel like my life. Now they feel like chores, or like I’m pretending to be someone I used to be.

We used to go dancing too, and even if I wasn’t the best dancer, I enjoyed being out with her.\n\nNow it’s like… I just can’t get myself there. Or if I do, I’m not really there. I sit there feeling like I’m waiting for it to be over. A lot of the time I make excuses — I’m tired, I’ve got something to do, maybe next time — but the truth is, none of it feels worth the effort. And then I feel bad, because these are things that used to feel like my life. Now they feel like chores, or like I’m pretending to be someone I used to be.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… yes. It’s not just at work. Work may have stirred it all up, or made it worse, but once I’m home it doesn’t just switch off.

Most days I wake up with it already there — this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. Like before I even get my feet on the floor, I can tell what kind of day it’s going to be. And it follows me around the house. I’ll be sitting at the kitchen table, or walking from one room to another, and there’s just this… emptiness. Like the old Ruben — the guy who could talk to anybody, who could get a little enjoyment out of the day — he’s just gone somewhere.

I keep thinking maybe if I’m away from work, I’ll feel more like myself, but I don’t. It’s there in the mornings, it’s there through most of the day, and even when nothing particular is happening, I still feel down.

This feels different enough that I keep measuring everything against it, and before this, I just don’t remember having a period that was clearly the same kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I had things I looked forward to.\n\nI used to love bowling — not even just the game, but the whole routine of it, seeing people, joking around, keeping score, talking trash a little. And the garden… I could spend half a Saturday out there messing with plants, pulling weeds, making little plans in my head about what I wanted to fix next. My wife and I would go to gallery shows sometimes, and I liked taking her, seeing her get excited about the paintings or whatever was there. We used to go dancing too, and even if I wasn’t the best dancer, I enjoyed being out with her.\n\nNow it’s like… I just can’t get myself there. Or if I do, I’m not really there. I sit there feeling like I’m waiting for it to be over. A lot of the time I make excuses — I’m tired, I’ve got something to do, maybe next time — but the truth is, none of it feels worth the effort. And then I feel bad, because these are things that used to feel like my life. Now they feel like chores, or like I’m pretending to be someone I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you still feel down most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. It’s not just at work. Work may have stirred it all up, or made it worse, but once I’m home it doesn’t just switch off.\n\nMost days I wake up with it already there — this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. Like before I even get my feet on the floor, I can tell what kind of day it’s going to be. And it follows me around the house. I’ll be sitting at the kitchen table, or walking from one room to another, and there’s just this… emptiness. Like the old Ruben — the guy who could talk to anybody, who could get a little enjoyment out of the day — he’s just gone somewhere.\n\nI keep thinking maybe if I’m away from work, I’ll feel more like myself, but I don’t. It’s there in the mornings, it’s there through most of the day, and even when nothing particular is happening, I still feel down.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo these feelings seem stronger than what you would normally expect from the problems at work?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I do think it’s stronger than what I’d expect, honestly. I mean, work has been hard, and I don’t want to pretend that hasn’t affected me. But this feels bigger than just having a bad job situation or being stressed after a rough day.

That’s why I came in, really. Most days I wake up and it’s already there — this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. It’s like before anything even happens, before work or conversations or whatever, I already feel weighed down. And I keep thinking about how I used to be… I could talk to anybody, I could get through the day and find some enjoyment in little things. Now it feels like that version of me is just gone.

So, yes, the problems at work may be part of it, but what I’m feeling doesn’t seem like a normal reaction anymore. It feels like it’s taken over too much of me.

That’s been my whole life, really, especially in sales. I could enjoy the little parts of the day, make conversation, joke around, feel like myself. It wasn’t like one single terrible day where everything changed overnight, but if I’m honest, it really started around eight months ago.\n\nThat’s when the new regional manager came in, and things at work just started getting… I don’t know, tighter and meaner. He started coming down on me a lot, questioning things I’d been doing successfully for years, and then he began taking accounts away from me — accounts I’d handled for a long time, people I’d built relationships with. In sales, that’s not just numbers on a sheet, you know? That’s your reputation, your rhythm, your whole sense that you still know what you’re doing.\n\nSo, yeah, I’d say that was the start of it. About eight months ago, after all that began.\n\nDoctor: If the problems at work were resolved, do you think this heavy, hollow feeling would go away?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say that’s the main thing I notice. I’m not sitting around making lists of worries all day, if that’s what you mean.

There are times I get uneasy, sure, but it’s hard for me to separate what’s worry from just feeling worn down and not right. I’ll catch myself thinking about things more than I used to, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. It’s more like… I don’t have a lot of confidence in anything feeling okay. But I’m not sure that’s the same as worrying a lot.

So I guess my honest answer is: maybe some, but I haven’t really been thinking of it that way. The sadness and the heaviness are more what I’ve been aware of.

It wasn’t like one single terrible day where everything changed overnight, but if I’m honest, it really started around eight months ago.\n\nThat’s when the new regional manager came in, and things at work just started getting… I don’t know, tighter and meaner. He started coming down on me a lot, questioning things I’d been doing successfully for years, and then he began taking accounts away from me — accounts I’d handled for a long time, people I’d built relationships with. In sales, that’s not just numbers on a sheet, you know? That’s your reputation, your rhythm, your whole sense that you still know what you’re doing.\n\nSo, yeah, I’d say that was the start of it. About eight months ago, after all that began.\n\nDoctor: If the problems at work were resolved, do you think this heavy, hollow feeling would go away?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. Part of me wants to believe that if the work situation got fixed, if people stopped questioning me and I felt like I had my footing again, then maybe I’d snap back into myself.\n\nBut honestly… I don’t know that it would just go away. Because this feeling is there before I even get to work, before I check my phone, before anything happens. I wake up and it’s already sitting in my chest — this heavy, hollow thing. Now it feels like that version of me is just gone.\n\nSo, yes, the problems at work may be part of it, but what I’m feeling doesn’t seem like a normal reaction anymore. It feels like it’s taken over too much of me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say that’s the main thing I notice. I’m not sitting around making lists of worries all day, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere are times I get uneasy, sure, but it’s hard for me to separate what’s worry from just feeling worn down and not right. I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean… I haven’t sat down and thought, “I can’t concentrate,” exactly.

I sit there feeling like I’m waiting for it to be over. A lot of the time I make excuses — I’m tired, I’ve got something to do, maybe next time — but the truth is, none of it feels worth the effort. And then I feel bad, because these are things that used to feel like my life. Now they feel like chores, or like I’m pretending to be someone I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you still feel down most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. It’s not just at work. Work may have stirred it all up, or made it worse, but once I’m home it doesn’t just switch off.\n\nMost days I wake up with it already there — this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. Like before I even get my feet on the floor, I can tell what kind of day it’s going to be. And it follows me around the house. I’ll be sitting at the kitchen table, or walking from one room to another, and there’s just this… emptiness. Like the old Ruben — the guy who could talk to anybody, who could get a little enjoyment out of the day — he’s just gone somewhere.\n\nI keep thinking maybe if I’m away from work, I’ll feel more like myself, but I don’t. It’s there in the mornings, it’s there through most of the day, and even when nothing particular is happening, I still feel down.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than what you would normally expect from the problems at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do think it’s stronger than what I’d expect, honestly. I mean, work has been hard, and I don’t want to pretend that hasn’t affected me. But this feels bigger than just having a bad job situation or being stressed after a rough day.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really. Most days I wake up and it’s already there — this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. It’s like before anything even happens, before work or conversations or whatever, I already feel weighed down. And I keep thinking about how I used to be… I could talk to anybody, I could get through the day and find some enjoyment in little things. Now it feels like that version of me is just gone.\n\nSo, yes, the problems at work may be part of it, but what I’m feeling doesn’t seem like a normal reaction anymore. It feels like it’s taken over too much of me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say that’s the main thing I notice. I’m not sitting around making lists of worries all day, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere are times I get uneasy, sure, but it’s hard for me to separate what’s worry from just feeling worn down and not right. I’ll catch myself thinking about things more than I used to, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. It’s more like… I don’t have a lot of confidence in anything feeling okay. But I’m not sure that’s the same as worrying a lot.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is: maybe some, but I haven’t really been thinking of it that way. The sadness and the heaviness are more what I’ve been aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean… I haven’t sat down and thought, “I can’t concentrate,” exactly.\n\nThere are times when I feel like my mind isn’t as sharp as it used to be, but I don’t know if that’s concentration or just being tired in my head, if that makes sense. Like I’ll start to do something and then sort of lose the thread for a second, but I haven’t been keeping track of it as a main problem.\n\nI guess I’d say I’ve noticed I don’t feel fully present a lot of the time, but I’m not sure whether I’d call it trouble concentrating. What do you mean exactly — like reading, watching TV, conversations, that kind of thing?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m not sure, honestly. “Tense or on edge” isn’t exactly the way I’ve been thinking about it.

I mean… I have moments where I feel unsettled, like I can’t quite relax into myself, but I don’t know if that’s being tense or if it’s just everything feeling heavy and off. I’m not pacing around or feeling like something terrible is about to happen all the time, at least not that I’ve noticed.

Sometimes my body does feel kind of tight, I guess, but then I wonder if I’m just noticing it because you asked. I haven’t really been walking around saying, “I’m on edge.” It’s more like I feel worn down and uncomfortable in my own skin, if that makes sense.

So… maybe a little at times, but I wouldn’t say it’s been one of the main things I’ve noticed.

In sales, that’s not just numbers on a sheet, you know? That’s your reputation, your rhythm, your whole sense that you still know what you’re doing.\n\nSo, yeah, I’d say that was the start of it. About eight months ago, after all that began.\n\nDoctor: If the problems at work were resolved, do you think this heavy, hollow feeling would go away?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. Part of me wants to believe that if the work situation got fixed, if people stopped questioning me and I felt like I had my footing again, then maybe I’d snap back into myself.\n\nBut honestly… I don’t know that it would just go away. Because this feeling is there before I even get to work, before I check my phone, before anything happens. I wake up and it’s already sitting in my chest — this heavy, hollow thing. And it’s not just “I’m upset about my job,” exactly. It feels more like the old me, the Ruben who could talk to anybody, who could enjoy the day and feel comfortable in his own skin… like that guy has disappeared somehow.\n\nSo, yeah, fixing work would probably help some. I’m sure it would. But I’m scared it wouldn’t be enough, because this feels like it’s gotten inside me.\n\nDoctor: Before these last eight months, had you already been feeling down like this for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No… no, not like this. I mean, I’ve had rough stretches in life like anybody, especially with work stress here and there, but this particular feeling — this heavy, hollow thing — it’s been about eight months.\n\nIt really lines up with when the new regional manager came in and started coming down on me, taking accounts away that I’d handled for years. That’s when I started feeling myself sink, little by little. Before that, I wouldn’t say I was feeling down like this for years. Not for two years, no.\n\nDoctor: Before this started eight months ago, had you ever gone through another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, not like this, not that I can clearly point to.\n\nI’ve had bad weeks before. Everybody does, I guess. Times where work was rough, or I felt worn down, or I didn’t feel like myself for a little while. But if you’re asking whether there was another real stretch where I felt depressed the way I’ve felt these past months — heavy like this, stuck like this — for two weeks or more… I don’t know that I can honestly say yes.\n\nNothing stands out in my mind the way this does. This feels different enough that I keep measuring everything against it, and before this, I just don’t remember having a period that was clearly the same kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No… not really. That’s actually one of the things that scares me, because I used to be pretty easy to please, you know? I had things I looked forward to.\n\nI used to love bowling — not even just the game, but the whole routine of it, seeing people, joking around, keeping score, talking trash a little. And the garden… I could spend half a Saturday out there messing with plants, pulling weeds, making little plans in my head about what I wanted to fix next. My wife and I would go to gallery shows sometimes, and I liked taking her, seeing her get excited about the paintings or whatever was there. We used to go dancing too, and even if I wasn’t the best dancer, I enjoyed being out with her.\n\nNow it’s like… I just can’t get myself there. Or if I do, I’m not really there. I sit there feeling like I’m waiting for it to be over. A lot of the time I make excuses — I’m tired, I’ve got something to do, maybe next time — but the truth is, none of it feels worth the effort. And then I feel bad, because these are things that used to feel like my life. Now they feel like chores, or like I’m pretending to be someone I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you still feel down most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. It’s not just at work. Work may have stirred it all up, or made it worse, but once I’m home it doesn’t just switch off.\n\nMost days I wake up with it already there — this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. Like before I even get my feet on the floor, I can tell what kind of day it’s going to be. And it follows me around the house. I’ll be sitting at the kitchen table, or walking from one room to another, and there’s just this… emptiness. Like the old Ruben — the guy who could talk to anybody, who could get a little enjoyment out of the day — he’s just gone somewhere.\n\nI keep thinking maybe if I’m away from work, I’ll feel more like myself, but I don’t. It’s there in the mornings, it’s there through most of the day, and even when nothing particular is happening, I still feel down.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than what you would normally expect from the problems at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do think it’s stronger than what I’d expect, honestly. I mean, work has been hard, and I don’t want to pretend that hasn’t affected me. But this feels bigger than just having a bad job situation or being stressed after a rough day.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really. Most days I wake up and it’s already there — this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. It’s like before anything even happens, before work or conversations or whatever, I already feel weighed down. And I keep thinking about how I used to be… I could talk to anybody, I could get through the day and find some enjoyment in little things. Now it feels like that version of me is just gone.\n\nSo, yes, the problems at work may be part of it, but what I’m feeling doesn’t seem like a normal reaction anymore. It feels like it’s taken over too much of me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say that’s the main thing I notice. I’m not sitting around making lists of worries all day, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere are times I get uneasy, sure, but it’s hard for me to separate what’s worry from just feeling worn down and not right. I’ll catch myself thinking about things more than I used to, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. It’s more like… I don’t have a lot of confidence in anything feeling okay. But I’m not sure that’s the same as worrying a lot.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is: maybe some, but I haven’t really been thinking of it that way. The sadness and the heaviness are more what I’ve been aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean… I haven’t sat down and thought, “I can’t concentrate,” exactly.\n\nThere are times when I feel like my mind isn’t as sharp as it used to be, but I don’t know if that’s concentration or just being tired in my head, if that makes sense. Like I’ll start to do something and then sort of lose the thread for a second, but I haven’t been keeping track of it as a main problem.\n\nI guess I’d say I’ve noticed I don’t feel fully present a lot of the time, but I’m not sure whether I’d call it trouble concentrating. What do you mean exactly — like reading, watching TV, conversations, that kind of thing?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling tense or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. “Tense or on edge” isn’t exactly the way I’ve been thinking about it.\n\nI mean… I have moments where I feel unsettled, like I can’t quite relax into myself, but I don’t know if that’s being tense or if it’s just everything feeling heavy and off. I’m not pacing around or feeling like something terrible is about to happen all the time, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nSometimes my body does feel kind of tight, I guess, but then I wonder if I’m just noticing it because you asked. I haven’t really been walking around saying, “I’m on edge.” It’s more like I feel worn down and uncomfortable in my own skin, if that makes sense.\n\nSo… maybe a little at times, but I wouldn’t say it’s been one of the main things I’ve noticed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Yeah… sleep has been pretty bad, actually. That’s one thing I do notice.

I’ll get into bed and I’m exhausted, like I feel like I should just drop off, but then I just lie there. I end up staring at the ceiling and my mind starts going back over work stuff — conversations I had, things I should’ve done differently, what’s waiting for me the next day. It’s like once the house is quiet, there’s nothing to distract me from it, and I just keep replaying things.

And then even when I do finally fall asleep, it doesn’t feel solid. A lot of nights I wake up around three or four in the morning, and I’m already tired. Not just groggy, but like… worn out before the day has even started. And then I start thinking about having to get up and go through everything again, and I just lie there dreading it.

So, yes. I guess I’d say I’m having a lot of trouble sleeping.

I’m not pacing around or feeling like something terrible is about to happen all the time, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nSometimes my body does feel kind of tight, I guess, but then I wonder if I’m just noticing it because you asked. I haven’t really been walking around saying, “I’m on edge.” It’s more like I feel worn down and uncomfortable in my own skin, if that makes sense.\n\nSo… maybe a little at times, but I wouldn’t say it’s been one of the main things I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been pretty bad, actually. I’m not really sure. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it the way someone might, like writing down meals or weighing myself or anything like that.

Some days I eat because it’s just the time to eat, not because I’m really thinking, “I’m hungry.” But I don’t know if that’s a change or just me not paying attention. Other times I’ll realize I skipped something or just picked at food, but then I wonder if that’s normal, you know, if I was busy or distracted or just didn’t feel like dealing with making anything.

I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some clear, dramatic change in my appetite. It’s more… I don’t feel especially tuned in to it. If you asked me what I ate yesterday, I’d have to think pretty hard. So I guess my honest answer is I don’t really know. I haven’t noticed it as a main thing.

It wasn’t like one single terrible day where everything changed overnight, but if I’m honest, it really started around eight months ago.\n\nThat’s when the new regional manager came in, and things at work just started getting… I don’t know, tighter and meaner. He started coming down on me a lot, questioning things I’d been doing successfully for years, and then he began taking accounts away from me — accounts I’d handled for a long time, people I’d built relationships with. In sales, that’s not just numbers on a sheet, you know? That’s your reputation, your rhythm, your whole sense that you still know what you’re doing.\n\nSo, yeah, I’d say that was the start of it. About eight months ago, after all that began.\n\nDoctor: If the problems at work were resolved, do you think this heavy, hollow feeling would go away?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. Part of me wants to believe that if the work situation got fixed, if people stopped questioning me and I felt like I had my footing again, then maybe I’d snap back into myself.\n\nBut honestly… I don’t know that it would just go away. Because this feeling is there before I even get to work, before I check my phone, before anything happens. I wake up and it’s already sitting in my chest — this heavy, hollow thing. And it’s not just “I’m upset about my job,” exactly. It feels more like the old me, the Ruben who could talk to anybody, who could enjoy the day and feel comfortable in his own skin… like that guy has disappeared somehow.\n\nSo, yeah, fixing work would probably help some. I’m sure it would. But I’m scared it wouldn’t be enough, because this feels like it’s gotten inside me.\n\nDoctor: Before these last eight months, had you already been feeling down like this for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No… no, not like this. I mean, I’ve had rough stretches in life like anybody, especially with work stress here and there, but this particular feeling — this heavy, hollow thing — it’s been about eight months.\n\nIt really lines up with when the new regional manager came in and started coming down on me, taking accounts away that I’d handled for years. That’s when I started feeling myself sink, little by little. Before that, I wouldn’t say I was feeling down like this for years. Not for two years, no.\n\nDoctor: Before this started eight months ago, had you ever gone through another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, not like this, not that I can clearly point to.\n\nI’ve had bad weeks before. Everybody does, I guess. Times where work was rough, or I felt worn down, or I didn’t feel like myself for a little while. But if you’re asking whether there was another real stretch where I felt depressed the way I’ve felt these past months — heavy like this, stuck like this — for two weeks or more… I don’t know that I can honestly say yes.\n\nNothing stands out in my mind the way this does. This feels different enough that I keep measuring everything against it, and before this, I just don’t remember having a period that was clearly the same kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No… not really. That’s actually one of the things that scares me, because I used to be pretty easy to please, you know? I had things I looked forward to.\n\nI used to love bowling — not even just the game, but the whole routine of it, seeing people, joking around, keeping score, talking trash a little. And the garden… I could spend half a Saturday out there messing with plants, pulling weeds, making little plans in my head about what I wanted to fix next. My wife and I would go to gallery shows sometimes, and I liked taking her, seeing her get excited about the paintings or whatever was there. We used to go dancing too, and even if I wasn’t the best dancer, I enjoyed being out with her.\n\nNow it’s like… I just can’t get myself there. Or if I do, I’m not really there. I sit there feeling like I’m waiting for it to be over. A lot of the time I make excuses — I’m tired, I’ve got something to do, maybe next time — but the truth is, none of it feels worth the effort. And then I feel bad, because these are things that used to feel like my life. Now they feel like chores, or like I’m pretending to be someone I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you still feel down most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. It’s not just at work. Work may have stirred it all up, or made it worse, but once I’m home it doesn’t just switch off.\n\nMost days I wake up with it already there — this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. Like before I even get my feet on the floor, I can tell what kind of day it’s going to be. And it follows me around the house. I’ll be sitting at the kitchen table, or walking from one room to another, and there’s just this… emptiness. Like the old Ruben — the guy who could talk to anybody, who could get a little enjoyment out of the day — he’s just gone somewhere.\n\nI keep thinking maybe if I’m away from work, I’ll feel more like myself, but I don’t. It’s there in the mornings, it’s there through most of the day, and even when nothing particular is happening, I still feel down.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than what you would normally expect from the problems at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do think it’s stronger than what I’d expect, honestly. I mean, work has been hard, and I don’t want to pretend that hasn’t affected me. But this feels bigger than just having a bad job situation or being stressed after a rough day.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really. Most days I wake up and it’s already there — this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. It’s like before anything even happens, before work or conversations or whatever, I already feel weighed down. And I keep thinking about how I used to be… I could talk to anybody, I could get through the day and find some enjoyment in little things. Now it feels like that version of me is just gone.\n\nSo, yes, the problems at work may be part of it, but what I’m feeling doesn’t seem like a normal reaction anymore. It feels like it’s taken over too much of me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say that’s the main thing I notice. I’m not sitting around making lists of worries all day, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere are times I get uneasy, sure, but it’s hard for me to separate what’s worry from just feeling worn down and not right. I’ll catch myself thinking about things more than I used to, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. It’s more like… I don’t have a lot of confidence in anything feeling okay. But I’m not sure that’s the same as worrying a lot.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is: maybe some, but I haven’t really been thinking of it that way. The sadness and the heaviness are more what I’ve been aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean… I haven’t sat down and thought, “I can’t concentrate,” exactly.\n\nThere are times when I feel like my mind isn’t as sharp as it used to be, but I don’t know if that’s concentration or just being tired in my head, if that makes sense. Like I’ll start to do something and then sort of lose the thread for a second, but I haven’t been keeping track of it as a main problem.\n\nI guess I’d say I’ve noticed I don’t feel fully present a lot of the time, but I’m not sure whether I’d call it trouble concentrating. What do you mean exactly — like reading, watching TV, conversations, that kind of thing?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling tense or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. “Tense or on edge” isn’t exactly the way I’ve been thinking about it.\n\nI mean… I have moments where I feel unsettled, like I can’t quite relax into myself, but I don’t know if that’s being tense or if it’s just everything feeling heavy and off. I’m not pacing around or feeling like something terrible is about to happen all the time, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nSometimes my body does feel kind of tight, I guess, but then I wonder if I’m just noticing it because you asked. I haven’t really been walking around saying, “I’m on edge.” It’s more like I feel worn down and uncomfortable in my own skin, if that makes sense.\n\nSo… maybe a little at times, but I wouldn’t say it’s been one of the main things I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been pretty bad, actually. That’s one thing I do notice.\n\nI’ll get into bed and I’m exhausted, like I feel like I should just drop off, but then I just lie there. I end up staring at the ceiling and my mind starts going back over work stuff — conversations I had, things I should’ve done differently, what’s waiting for me the next day. It’s like once the house is quiet, there’s nothing to distract me from it, and I just keep replaying things.\n\nAnd then even when I do finally fall asleep, it doesn’t feel solid. A lot of nights I wake up around three or four in the morning, and I’m already tired. Not just groggy, but like… worn out before the day has even started. And then I start thinking about having to get up and go through everything again, and I just lie there dreading it.\n\nSo, yes. I guess I’d say I’m having a lot of trouble sleeping.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it the way someone might, like writing down meals or weighing myself or anything like that.\n\nSome days I eat because it’s just the time to eat, not because I’m really thinking, “I’m hungry.” But I don’t know if that’s a change or just me not paying attention. Other times I’ll realize I skipped something or just picked at food, but then I wonder if that’s normal, you know, if I was busy or distracted or just didn’t feel like dealing with making anything.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some clear, dramatic change in my appetite. It’s more… I don’t feel especially tuned in to it. If you asked me what I ate yesterday, I’d have to think pretty hard. So I guess my honest answer is I don’t really know. I haven’t noticed it as a main thing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you been feeling tense or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. “Tense or on edge” isn’t exactly the way I’ve been thinking about it.\n\nI mean… I have moments where I feel unsettled, like I can’t quite relax into myself, but I don’t know if that’s being tense or if it’s just everything feeling heavy and off. I’m not pacing around or feeling like something terrible is about to happen all the time, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nSometimes my body does feel kind of tight, I guess, but then I wonder if I’m just noticing it because you asked. I haven’t really been walking around saying, “I’m on edge.” It’s more like I feel worn down and uncomfortable in my own skin, if that makes sense.\n\nSo… maybe a little at times, but I wouldn’t say it’s been one of the main things I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been pretty bad, actually. That’s one thing I do notice.\n\nI’ll get into bed and I’m exhausted, like I feel like I should just drop off, but then I just lie there. I end up staring at the ceiling and my mind starts going back over work stuff — conversations I had, things I should’ve done differently, what’s waiting for me the next day. It’s like once the house is quiet, there’s nothing to distract me from it, and I just keep replaying things.\n\nAnd then even when I do finally fall asleep, it doesn’t feel solid. A lot of nights I wake up around three or four in the morning, and I’m already tired. Not just groggy, but like… worn out before the day has even started. And then I start thinking about having to get up and go through everything again, and I just lie there dreading it.\n\nSo, yes. I guess I’d say I’m having a lot of trouble sleeping.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it the way someone might, like writing down meals or weighing myself or anything like that.\n\nSome days I eat because it’s just the time to eat, not because I’m really thinking, “I’m hungry.” But I don’t know if that’s a change or just me not paying attention. Other times I’ll realize I skipped something or just picked at food, but then I wonder if that’s normal, you know, if I was busy or distracted or just didn’t feel like dealing with making anything.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some clear, dramatic change in my appetite. It’s more… I don’t feel especially tuned in to it. If you asked me what I ate yesterday, I’d have to think pretty hard. So I guess my honest answer is I don’t really know. I haven’t noticed it as a main thing.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 58 / sales_representative_of_services / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t really know how to answer that clearly. I mean, I feel worn out a lot, but I’m not sure if I’d call it “unusually tired” or low energy in the way you mean.

I’ve been moving through the day kind of slowly, I guess, but it’s hard to separate that from just not sleeping well or not feeling right overall. At my age, and with work being what it is, I don’t know what counts as unusual anymore. Some days I think, “Of course I’m tired,” but then other days I wonder if I’m just making excuses for myself.

It’s not like I’m collapsing or anything like that. I still get up and do what I’m supposed to do, mostly. But it takes more effort than I’d like to admit. Even small things can feel like they require me to talk myself into them.

So… yes, maybe, but I don’t have a clean answer. I’ve noticed feeling drained, but I haven’t really been measuring it or thinking of it as its own separate problem.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.9, F43.2, F41.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… thanks. I, uh… I came in because most days lately I wake up with this heavy, hollow kind of feeling right here in my chest. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic, but it’s like I open my eyes and before anything even happens, before the day has a chance to go wrong, it’s already there.\n\nI keep thinking about how I used to be — I mean, I’m Ruben, I’ve always been the guy who could talk to anybody, you know? That’s been my whole life, really, especially in sales. I could enjoy the little parts of the day, make conversation, joke around, feel like myself. And now it feels like that old version of me is just… gone. Like there’s this empty space where he used to be. That’s what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after something stressful or upsetting happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I think so. It wasn’t like one single terrible day where everything changed overnight, but if I’m honest, it really started around eight months ago.\n\nThat’s when the new regional manager came in, and things at work just started getting… I don’t know, tighter and meaner. He started coming down on me a lot, questioning things I’d been doing successfully for years, and then he began taking accounts away from me — accounts I’d handled for a long time, people I’d built relationships with. In sales, that’s not just numbers on a sheet, you know? That’s your reputation, your rhythm, your whole sense that you still know what you’re doing.\n\nSo, yeah, I’d say that was the start of it. About eight months ago, after all that began.\n\nDoctor: If the problems at work were resolved, do you think this heavy, hollow feeling would go away?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. Part of me wants to believe that if the work situation got fixed, if people stopped questioning me and I felt like I had my footing again, then maybe I’d snap back into myself.\n\nBut honestly… I don’t know that it would just go away. Because this feeling is there before I even get to work, before I check my phone, before anything happens. I wake up and it’s already sitting in my chest — this heavy, hollow thing. And it’s not just “I’m upset about my job,” exactly. It feels more like the old me, the Ruben who could talk to anybody, who could enjoy the day and feel comfortable in his own skin… like that guy has disappeared somehow.\n\nSo, yeah, fixing work would probably help some. I’m sure it would. But I’m scared it wouldn’t be enough, because this feels like it’s gotten inside me.\n\nDoctor: Before these last eight months, had you already been feeling down like this for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No… no, not like this. I mean, I’ve had rough stretches in life like anybody, especially with work stress here and there, but this particular feeling — this heavy, hollow thing — it’s been about eight months.\n\nIt really lines up with when the new regional manager came in and started coming down on me, taking accounts away that I’d handled for years. That’s when I started feeling myself sink, little by little. Before that, I wouldn’t say I was feeling down like this for years. Not for two years, no.\n\nDoctor: Before this started eight months ago, had you ever gone through another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, not like this, not that I can clearly point to.\n\nI’ve had bad weeks before. Everybody does, I guess. Times where work was rough, or I felt worn down, or I didn’t feel like myself for a little while. But if you’re asking whether there was another real stretch where I felt depressed the way I’ve felt these past months — heavy like this, stuck like this — for two weeks or more… I don’t know that I can honestly say yes.\n\nNothing stands out in my mind the way this does. This feels different enough that I keep measuring everything against it, and before this, I just don’t remember having a period that was clearly the same kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No… not really. That’s actually one of the things that scares me, because I used to be pretty easy to please, you know? I had things I looked forward to.\n\nI used to love bowling — not even just the game, but the whole routine of it, seeing people, joking around, keeping score, talking trash a little. And the garden… I could spend half a Saturday out there messing with plants, pulling weeds, making little plans in my head about what I wanted to fix next. My wife and I would go to gallery shows sometimes, and I liked taking her, seeing her get excited about the paintings or whatever was there. We used to go dancing too, and even if I wasn’t the best dancer, I enjoyed being out with her.\n\nNow it’s like… I just can’t get myself there. Or if I do, I’m not really there. I sit there feeling like I’m waiting for it to be over. A lot of the time I make excuses — I’m tired, I’ve got something to do, maybe next time — but the truth is, none of it feels worth the effort. And then I feel bad, because these are things that used to feel like my life. Now they feel like chores, or like I’m pretending to be someone I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you still feel down most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. It’s not just at work. Work may have stirred it all up, or made it worse, but once I’m home it doesn’t just switch off.\n\nMost days I wake up with it already there — this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. Like before I even get my feet on the floor, I can tell what kind of day it’s going to be. And it follows me around the house. I’ll be sitting at the kitchen table, or walking from one room to another, and there’s just this… emptiness. Like the old Ruben — the guy who could talk to anybody, who could get a little enjoyment out of the day — he’s just gone somewhere.\n\nI keep thinking maybe if I’m away from work, I’ll feel more like myself, but I don’t. It’s there in the mornings, it’s there through most of the day, and even when nothing particular is happening, I still feel down.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than what you would normally expect from the problems at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do think it’s stronger than what I’d expect, honestly. I mean, work has been hard, and I don’t want to pretend that hasn’t affected me. But this feels bigger than just having a bad job situation or being stressed after a rough day.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really. Most days I wake up and it’s already there — this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. It’s like before anything even happens, before work or conversations or whatever, I already feel weighed down. And I keep thinking about how I used to be… I could talk to anybody, I could get through the day and find some enjoyment in little things. Now it feels like that version of me is just gone.\n\nSo, yes, the problems at work may be part of it, but what I’m feeling doesn’t seem like a normal reaction anymore. It feels like it’s taken over too much of me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say that’s the main thing I notice. I’m not sitting around making lists of worries all day, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere are times I get uneasy, sure, but it’s hard for me to separate what’s worry from just feeling worn down and not right. I’ll catch myself thinking about things more than I used to, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. It’s more like… I don’t have a lot of confidence in anything feeling okay. But I’m not sure that’s the same as worrying a lot.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is: maybe some, but I haven’t really been thinking of it that way. The sadness and the heaviness are more what I’ve been aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean… I haven’t sat down and thought, “I can’t concentrate,” exactly.\n\nThere are times when I feel like my mind isn’t as sharp as it used to be, but I don’t know if that’s concentration or just being tired in my head, if that makes sense. Like I’ll start to do something and then sort of lose the thread for a second, but I haven’t been keeping track of it as a main problem.\n\nI guess I’d say I’ve noticed I don’t feel fully present a lot of the time, but I’m not sure whether I’d call it trouble concentrating. What do you mean exactly — like reading, watching TV, conversations, that kind of thing?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling tense or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. “Tense or on edge” isn’t exactly the way I’ve been thinking about it.\n\nI mean… I have moments where I feel unsettled, like I can’t quite relax into myself, but I don’t know if that’s being tense or if it’s just everything feeling heavy and off. I’m not pacing around or feeling like something terrible is about to happen all the time, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nSometimes my body does feel kind of tight, I guess, but then I wonder if I’m just noticing it because you asked. I haven’t really been walking around saying, “I’m on edge.” It’s more like I feel worn down and uncomfortable in my own skin, if that makes sense.\n\nSo… maybe a little at times, but I wouldn’t say it’s been one of the main things I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been pretty bad, actually. That’s one thing I do notice.\n\nI’ll get into bed and I’m exhausted, like I feel like I should just drop off, but then I just lie there. I end up staring at the ceiling and my mind starts going back over work stuff — conversations I had, things I should’ve done differently, what’s waiting for me the next day. It’s like once the house is quiet, there’s nothing to distract me from it, and I just keep replaying things.\n\nAnd then even when I do finally fall asleep, it doesn’t feel solid. A lot of nights I wake up around three or four in the morning, and I’m already tired. Not just groggy, but like… worn out before the day has even started. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I have. More than I’d like to admit.

It’s not just feeling bad about a mistake here or there. It’s more like this voice in my head keeps telling me I’m useless now. Like all those years where I was taking care of clients, keeping things going, providing for my family — it’s like none of that counts anymore because right now I can’t even seem to handle this job the way I used to.

And I know, if someone else said that to me, I’d probably tell them that’s not fair, that one rough stretch doesn’t erase a whole life of work. But when it’s me, I don’t seem to give myself that same break. I keep thinking, “What good are you if you can’t do the thing you’re supposed to do?” It just goes around and around.

So yes, I guess I do blame myself a lot. And I feel… pretty worthless sometimes.

Yeah… I have. I don’t like saying that out loud, but yes.

It’s not like I’m sitting there making some big announcement in my head, exactly. It’s more… I’ll be lying there or driving or just sitting somewhere, and this thought comes in that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Like maybe my wife and kids would be better off without having to deal with me being like this.

And I know that sounds awful. I know if one of them said that, I’d be terrified and I’d tell them they were wrong. But when it’s me thinking it, it feels different in the moment. It feels like I’m a burden, like I’m taking up space and making everything harder.

The part that scares me is that I don’t trust myself the way I used to. I’m not saying that lightly. I’ve always thought of myself as someone who could keep a handle on things, who could push through and be responsible. But lately, when those thoughts come up, they feel too close, if that makes sense. They scare me.

This feels different enough that I keep measuring everything against it, and before this, I just don’t remember having a period that was clearly the same kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No… not really. Like before I even get my feet on the floor, I can tell what kind of day it’s going to be. And it follows me around the house. I’ll be sitting at the kitchen table, or walking from one room to another, and there’s just this… emptiness. Like the old Ruben — the guy who could talk to anybody, who could get a little enjoyment out of the day — he’s just gone somewhere.\n\nI keep thinking maybe if I’m away from work, I’ll feel more like myself, but I don’t. Most days I wake up and it’s already there — this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. It’s like before anything even happens, before work or conversations or whatever, I already feel weighed down. I’ll catch myself thinking about things more than I used to, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. It’s more like… I don’t have a lot of confidence in anything feeling okay. The sadness and the heaviness are more what I’ve been aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. Like I’ll start to do something and then sort of lose the thread for a second, but I haven’t been keeping track of it as a main problem.\n\nI guess I’d say I’ve noticed I don’t feel fully present a lot of the time, but I’m not sure whether I’d call it trouble concentrating. What do you mean exactly — like reading, watching TV, conversations, that kind of thing?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling tense or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. “Tense or on edge” isn’t exactly the way I’ve been thinking about it.\n\nI mean… I have moments where I feel unsettled, like I can’t quite relax into myself, but I don’t know if that’s being tense or if it’s just everything feeling heavy and off. I haven’t really been walking around saying, “I’m on edge.” It’s more like I feel worn down and uncomfortable in my own skin, if that makes sense.\n\nSo… maybe a little at times, but I wouldn’t say it’s been one of the main things I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been pretty bad, actually. That’s one thing I do notice.\n\nI’ll get into bed and I’m exhausted, like I feel like I should just drop off, but then I just lie there. I end up staring at the ceiling and my mind starts going back over work stuff — conversations I had, things I should’ve done differently, what’s waiting for me the next day. It’s like once the house is quiet, there’s nothing to distract me from it, and I just keep replaying things.\n\nAnd then even when I do finally fall asleep, it doesn’t feel solid. It’s more… I don’t feel especially tuned in to it. If you asked me what I ate yesterday, I’d have to think pretty hard. So I guess my honest answer is I don’t really know. I haven’t noticed it as a main thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that clearly. I mean, I feel worn out a lot, but I’m not sure if I’d call it “unusually tired” or low energy in the way you mean.\n\nI’ve been moving through the day kind of slowly, I guess, but it’s hard to separate that from just not sleeping well or not feeling right overall. At my age, and with work being what it is, I don’t know what counts as unusual anymore. Some days I think, “Of course I’m tired,” but then other days I wonder if I’m just making excuses for myself.\n\nIt’s not like I’m collapsing or anything like that. I still get up and do what I’m supposed to do, mostly. But it takes more effort than I’d like to admit. Even small things can feel like they require me to talk myself into them.\n\nSo… yes, maybe, but I don’t have a clean answer. I’ve noticed feeling drained, but I haven’t really been measuring it or thinking of it as its own separate problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or blaming yourself a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. More than I’d like to admit.\n\nIt’s not just feeling bad about a mistake here or there. It’s more like this voice in my head keeps telling me I’m useless now. Like all those years where I was taking care of clients, keeping things going, providing for my family — it’s like none of that counts anymore because right now I can’t even seem to handle this job the way I used to.\n\nAnd I know, if someone else said that to me, I’d probably tell them that’s not fair, that one rough stretch doesn’t erase a whole life of work. But when it’s me, I don’t seem to give myself that same break. I keep thinking, “What good are you if you can’t do the thing you’re supposed to do?” It just goes around and around.\n\nSo yes, I guess I do blame myself a lot. And I feel… pretty worthless sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t like saying that out loud, but yes.\n\nIt’s not like I’m sitting there making some big announcement in my head, exactly. It’s more… I’ll be lying there or driving or just sitting somewhere, and this thought comes in that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Like maybe my wife and kids would be better off without having to deal with me being like this.\n\nAnd I know that sounds awful. I know if one of them said that, I’d be terrified and I’d tell them they were wrong. But when it’s me thinking it, it feels different in the moment. It feels like I’m a burden, like I’m taking up space and making everything harder.\n\nThe part that scares me is that I don’t trust myself the way I used to. I’m not saying that lightly. I’ve always thought of myself as someone who could keep a handle on things, who could push through and be responsible. But lately, when those thoughts come up, they feel too close, if that makes sense. They scare me."
